                                           *cli-preload-index-watchdog-config*

--watchdog-config

Configuration for the stack-dump watchdog.

API docs: |vllm.config.WatchdogConfig|

Should either be a valid JSON string or JSON keys passed individually.

Default: `WatchdogConfig(timeout=300, check_interval=10, dump_dir='')`

                                        *cli-preload-index-optimization-level*

--optimization-level

The optimization level. These levels trade startup time cost for
performance, with -O0 having the best startup time and -O3 having the
best performance. -O2 is used by default. See OptimizationLevel for full
description.

Default: `2`

                                          *cli-preload-index-performance-mode*

--performance-mode

Possible choices: `balanced`, `interactivity`, `throughput`

Performance mode for runtime behavior, 'balanced' is the default.
'interactivity' favors low end-to-end per-request latency at small batch
sizes (fine-grained CUDA graphs, latency-oriented kernels). 'throughput'
favors aggregate tokens/sec at high concurrency (larger CUDA graphs,
more aggressive batching, throughput-oriented kernels).

Default: `balanced`

                                    *cli-preload-index-weight-transfer-config*

--weight-transfer-config

The configurations for weight transfer during RL training.

API docs: |vllm.config.WeightTransferConfig|

Should either be a valid JSON string or JSON keys passed individually.

----------------------------------------------------------------------------
vllm run-batch                                      *vllm-cli-run-batch-index*

JSON CLI ARGUMENTS                    *cli-run-batch-index-json-cli-arguments*

When passing JSON CLI arguments, the following sets of arguments are
equivalent:

- `--json-arg '{"key1": "value1", "key2": {"key3": "value2"}}'`
- `--json-arg.key1 value1 --json-arg.key2.key3 value2`

Additionally, list elements can be passed individually using `+`:

- `--json-arg '{"key4": ["value3", "value4", "value5"]}'`
- `--json-arg.key4+ value3 --json-arg.key4+='value4,value5'`

ARGUMENTS                                      *cli-run-batch-index-arguments*

                                       *cli-run-batch-index-disable-log-stats*

--disable-log-stats

Disable logging statistics.

Default: `False`

                                *cli-run-batch-index-aggregate-engine-logging*

--aggregate-engine-logging

Log aggregate rather than per-engine statistics when using data
parallelism.

Default: `False`

*cli-run-batch-index-fail-on-environ-validation-no-fail-on-environ-validation*
`--fail-on-environ-validation`, `--no-fail-on-environ-validation`

If set, the engine will raise an error if environment validation fails.

Default: `False`

                                        *cli-run-batch-index-shutdown-timeout*

--shutdown-timeout

Shutdown timeout in seconds. 0 = abort, >0 = wait.

Default: `0`

                                     *cli-run-batch-index-gdn-prefill-backend*

--gdn-prefill-backend

Possible choices: `flashinfer`, `triton`, `cutedsl`, `aiter_flydsl`,
`sycl`, `hip`

Select GDN prefill backend.

                                     *cli-run-batch-index-kda-prefill-backend*

--kda-prefill-backend

Possible choices: `auto`, `triton`, `flashkda`, `flashinfer`, `fused`

Select KDA prefill backend. 'flashkda' is CUDA-only and 'fused' is
ROCm-only; 'auto' picks a supported backend.

                                      *cli-run-batch-index-kda-decode-backend*

--kda-decode-backend

Possible choices: `auto`, `native`, `flashinfer`, `triton`

Select KDA decode backend.

                *cli-run-batch-index-enable-log-requests-disable-log-requests*
`--enable-log-requests`, `--disable-log-requests`

Enable logging request information, dependent on log level: - INFO:
Request ID, parameters and LoRA request. - DEBUG: Prompt inputs (e.g:
text, token IDs). You can set the minimum log level via
`VLLM_LOGGING_LEVEL`. You can also set a separate log level for request
JSON body via `VLLM_REQUEST_BODY_LOG_LEVEL` (default: DEBUG).

Default: `False`

BatchFrontend                              *cli-run-batch-index-batchfrontend*

Arguments for the batch runner frontend.

                                            *cli-run-batch-index-lora-modules*

--lora-modules

LoRA modules configurations in either 'name=path' format or JSON format
or JSON list format. Example (old format): `'name=path'` Example (new
format):
`{"name": "name", "path": "lora_path", "base_model_name": "id"}`

                                           *cli-run-batch-index-chat-template*

--chat-template

The file path to the chat template, or the template in single-line form
for the specified model.

                            *cli-run-batch-index-chat-template-content-format*
`--chat-template-content-format`

Possible choices: `auto`, `openai`, `string`

The format to render message content within a chat template.

- "string" will render the content as a string. Example: `"Hello World"`
- "openai" will render the content as a list of dictionaries, similar to
  OpenAI schema. Example: `[{"type": "text", "text": "Hello world!"}]`

Default: `auto`

*cli-run-batch-index-trust-request-chat-template-no-trust-request-chat-template*
`--trust-request-chat-template`, `--no-trust-request-chat-template`

Whether to trust the chat template provided in the request. If False,
the server will always use the chat template specified by
`--chat-template` or the ones from tokenizer.

Default: `False`

      *cli-run-batch-index-trust-request-mm-kwargs-no-trust-request-mm-kwargs*
`--trust-request-mm-kwargs`, `--no-trust-request-mm-kwargs`

Whether to trust per-request multimodal kwargs (`mm_processor_kwargs`
and `media_io_kwargs`). If False, the server rejects non-empty values
because they can change multimodal preprocessing resource usage. Only
enable this when API clients are trusted.

Default: `False`

                            *cli-run-batch-index-default-chat-template-kwargs*
`--default-chat-template-kwargs`

Default keyword arguments to pass to the chat template renderer. These
will be merged with request-level chat_template_kwargs, with request
values taking precedence. Useful for setting default behavior for
reasoning models. Example: '{"enable_thinking": false}' to disable
thinking mode by default for Qwen3/DeepSeek models.

Should either be a valid JSON string or JSON keys passed individually.

                                    *cli-run-batch-index-reasoning-effort-map*

--reasoning-effort-map

JSON mapping that rewrites a request's reasoning effort to a value the
chat template supports, for templates that silently fall back on efforts
they do not recognize. Applies to the effort set through the Chat
Completions, Responses and Anthropic Messages APIs; efforts not listed
pass through unchanged. Example: '{"medium": "low", "xhigh": "max"}' for
a template that only knows low, high and max.

Should either be a valid JSON string or JSON keys passed individually.

                                           *cli-run-batch-index-response-role*

--response-role

The role name to return if `request.add_generation_prompt=true`.

Default: `assistant`

*cli-run-batch-index-return-tokens-as-token-ids-no-return-tokens-as-token-ids*
`--return-tokens-as-token-ids`, `--no-return-tokens-as-token-ids`

When `--max-logprobs` is specified, represents single tokens as strings
of the form 'token_id:{token_id}' so that tokens that are not
JSON-encodable can be identified.

Default: `False`

        *cli-run-batch-index-enable-auto-tool-choice-disable-auto-tool-choice*
`--enable-auto-tool-choice`, `--disable-auto-tool-choice`

Enable auto tool choice for supported models. Use `--tool-call-parser`
to specify which parser to use.

Default: `False`

*cli-run-batch-index-exclude-tools-when-tool-choice-none-no-exclude-tools-when-tool-choice-none*
`--exclude-tools-when-tool-choice-none`,
`--no-exclude-tools-when-tool-choice-none`

If specified, exclude tool definitions in prompts when
tool_choice='none'.

Default: `False`

                                        *cli-run-batch-index-tool-call-parser*

--tool-call-parser

Select the tool call parser depending on the model that you're using.
This is used to parse the model-generated tool call into OpenAI API
format. Required for `--enable-auto-tool-choice`. You can choose any
option from the built-in parsers or register a plugin via
`--tool-parser-plugin`.

                                      *cli-run-batch-index-tool-parser-plugin*

--tool-parser-plugin

Special the tool parser plugin write to parse the model-generated tool
into OpenAI API format, the name register in this plugin can be used in
`--tool-call-parser`.

Default: `""`

                                       *cli-run-batch-index-tool-strict-level*

--tool-strict-level

Possible choices: `auto`, `function`, `parameter`, `None`

Server-side floor for structural-tag based tool calling, applied on top
of the per-tool `strict` field. `auto` follows the request's tool choice
and per-tool strictness; `function` constrains the tool-call envelope
(markup and function name) for every request with tools; `parameter`
additionally pins argument schemas, as if every tool were
`strict: true`. When unset, the tool parser's default level applies,
which is `auto` for most parsers.

                                             *cli-run-batch-index-tool-server*

--tool-server

Comma-separated list of host:port pairs (IPv4, IPv6, or hostname).
Examples: 127.0.0.1:8000, [::1]:8000, localhost:1234. Or `demo` for
built-in demo tools (browser and Python code interpreter). WARNING: The
`demo` Python tool executes model-generated code in Docker without
network isolation by default. See the security guide for more
information.

                                             *cli-run-batch-index-max-log-len*

--max-log-len

Max number of prompt characters or prompt ID numbers being printed in
log. The default of None means unlimited.

*cli-run-batch-index-enable-prompt-tokens-details-disable-prompt-tokens-details*
`--enable-prompt-tokens-details`, `--disable-prompt-tokens-details`

If set to True, enable prompt_tokens_details in usage.

Default: `False`

  *cli-run-batch-index-enable-per-request-metrics-disable-per-request-metrics*
`--enable-per-request-metrics`, `--disable-per-request-metrics`

If set to True, include per-request timing metrics in API responses.

Default: `False`

*cli-run-batch-index-enable-server-load-tracking-disable-server-load-tracking*
`--enable-server-load-tracking`, `--disable-server-load-tracking`

If set to True, enable tracking server_load_metrics in the app state.

Default: `False`

  *cli-run-batch-index-enable-force-include-usage-disable-force-include-usage*
`--enable-force-include-usage`, `--disable-force-include-usage`

If set to True, including usage on every request.

Default: `False`

                                 *cli-run-batch-index-sse-keep-alive-interval*

--sse-keep-alive-interval

Send an SSE keep-alive comment line every this many seconds when a
`/v1/chat/completions` or `/v1/completions` streaming response is idle
(queued, prefill, or between tokens), to prevent reverse proxies/tunnels
with read timeouts from closing the connection. Defaults to 0, which
disables keep-alive comments entirely.

Default: `0`

*cli-run-batch-index-enable-tokenizer-info-endpoint-disable-tokenizer-info-endpoint*
`--enable-tokenizer-info-endpoint`, `--disable-tokenizer-info-endpoint`

Enable the `/tokenizer_info` endpoint. May expose chat templates and
other tokenizer configuration.

Default: `False`

                  *cli-run-batch-index-enable-log-outputs-disable-log-outputs*
`--enable-log-outputs`, `--disable-log-outputs`

If set to True, log model outputs (generations). Requires
`--enable-log-requests`. Output text and finish reasons are logged at
INFO, while output token IDs are logged at DEBUG.

Default: `False`

                    *cli-run-batch-index-enable-log-deltas-disable-log-deltas*
`--enable-log-deltas`, `--disable-log-deltas`

If set to False, output deltas will not be logged. Relevant only if
--enable-log-outputs is set.

Default: `True`

  *cli-run-batch-index-cohere-is-reasoning-model-no-cohere-is-reasoning-model*
`--cohere-is-reasoning-model`, `--no-cohere-is-reasoning-model`

Cohere `/cohere/v2/chat` only. Whether the served model is a reasoning
Command-family model. When True (default), the assistant's
chain-of-thought is surfaced as a `thinking` content block (or
`content-*` events on the stream). When False, reasoning is surfaced as
Cohere's `tool_plan` field (or `tool-plan-delta` events) whenever the
model emits tool calls, matching older non- reasoning Command models.
Has no effect on the non-Cohere endpoints.

Default: `True`

                                           *cli-run-batch-index-cohere-format*

--cohere-format

Cohere `--tokenizer-mode cohere` only. Which Cohere prompt format to
render: `cmd4` (current Command A+ models; default) or `cmd3` (earlier
Cmd-A and Cmd-A reasoning models). Selecting the wrong format silently
produces a prompt the model wasn't trained on, which most commonly
manifests as the model emitting text but no citations / tool calls /
thinking blocks. Equivalent to passing
`--default-chat-template-kwargs '{"cohere_format": "..."}'` -- any
explicit request-level `chat_template_kwargs.cohere_format` takes
priority.

Default: `cmd4`

                      *cli-run-batch-index-anthropic-disabled-thinking-effort*
`--anthropic-disabled-thinking-effort`

Possible choices: `auto`, `high`, `low`, `max`, `medium`, `none`,
`xhigh`

The `reasoning_effort` used for Anthropic Messages, token counting, and
render requests with `thinking: {"type": "disabled"}` or
`thinking: {"type": "between_tools"}`. `none` turns thinking off for
models that support it; `low` suits models that always think (e.g.
GLM-5.3) or reject `none` (e.g. gpt-oss). `auto` (default) uses `low`
when the renderer rejects `none` or renders it the same as a thinking
effort, and `none` otherwise.

Default: `auto`

                      *cli-run-batch-index-log-error-stack-no-log-error-stack*
`--log-error-stack`, `--no-log-error-stack`

If set to True, log the stack trace of error responses

Default: `False`

                              *cli-run-batch-index-tokens-only-no-tokens-only*
`--tokens-only`, `--no-tokens-only`

If set to True, only enable the Tokens In<>Out endpoint. This is
intended for use in a Disaggregated Everything setup.

Default: `False`

                      *cli-run-batch-index-enable-scale-out-disable-scale-out*
`--enable-scale-out`, `--disable-scale-out`

If set to True, register the scale-out endpoints (`/render`,
`/derender`, `/inference/v1/generate` and
`/inference/v1/abort_requests`) on `vllm serve`. Defaults to
`VLLM_SERVER_DEV_MODE`. Has no effect on `vllm launch render` or
`vllm serve --tokens-only` which always register their required
endpoints regardless of this flag.

Default: `False`

                                        *cli-run-batch-index-fingerprint-mode*

--fingerprint-mode

Possible choices: `custom`, `full`, `hash`, `none`

Controls the `system_fingerprint` field on responses.

- `full` (default): `vllm-<version>[-<parallelism>]-<hash8>`. Encodes
  server version, non-trivial parallelism degrees (tp/pp/dp/ep), and an
  8-char config hash.
- `hash`: `vllm-<version>-<hash8>`. Parallelism stripped.
- `custom`: emits the literal string from `--fingerprint-value`.
- `none`: the field is omitted (serialized as `null`).

Default: `full`

                                       *cli-run-batch-index-fingerprint-value*

--fingerprint-value

Literal fingerprint string used when `--fingerprint-mode=custom`.

                                     *cli-run-batch-index-max-tokenize-tokens*

--max-tokenize-tokens

Maximum number of tokens allowed in `/tokenize`, `/detokenize`, and
`/v1/messages/count_tokens` requests. Defaults to
`VLLM_MAX_TOKENIZE_TOKENS` if set, otherwise
`max(max_model_len * 2, 131072)`. Set to `<= 0` to disable the limit.
Currently enforced on standard template rendering paths in the Python
frontend (GPT-OSS Harmony rendering is not yet covered).

`-i`, `--input-file`                        *cli-run-batch-index-i-input-file*

The path or url to a single input file. Currently supports local file
paths, or the http protocol (http or https). If a URL is specified, the
file should be available via HTTP GET.

`-o`, `--output-file`                      *cli-run-batch-index-o-output-file*

The path or url to a single output file. Currently supports local file
paths, or web (http or https) urls. If a URL is specified, the file
should be available via HTTP PUT.

                                          *cli-run-batch-index-output-tmp-dir*

--output-tmp-dir

Directory for staging a URL input, a piped input, or the output before
it is uploaded to a URL. When unset, piped input and output are held in
memory and URL input is downloaded to the system temporary directory.

                                            *cli-run-batch-index-max-inflight*

--max-inflight

Maximum number of requests queued at the engine at once. Responses are
written in input order, so up to 16 times this many finished responses
may be held behind a slow request. Defaults to twice max_num_seqs across
all data-parallel engines, at least 1024. --max-num-queued-reqs and
--max-num-queued-tokens are ignored.

                                          *cli-run-batch-index-enable-metrics*

--enable-metrics

Enable Prometheus metrics

Default: `False`

                                                    *cli-run-batch-index-host*

--host

Host name for the Prometheus metrics server (only needed if
enable-metrics is set).

                                                    *cli-run-batch-index-port*

--port

Port number for the Prometheus metrics server (only needed if
enable-metrics is set).

Default: `8000`

                                                     *cli-run-batch-index-url*

--url

[DEPRECATED] Host name for the Prometheus metrics server (only needed if
enable-metrics is set). Use --host instead.

Default: `0.0.0.0`

ModelConfig                                  *cli-run-batch-index-modelconfig*

Configuration for the model.

                                                   *cli-run-batch-index-model*

--model

Name or path of the Hugging Face model to use. It is also used as the
content for `model_name` tag in metrics output when `served_model_name`
is not specified.

Default: `Qwen/Qwen3-0.6B`

                                                  *cli-run-batch-index-runner*

--runner

Possible choices: `auto`, `draft`, `generate`, `pooling`

The type of model runner to use. Each vLLM instance only supports one
model runner, even if the same model can be used for multiple types.

Default: `auto`

                                                 *cli-run-batch-index-convert*

--convert

Possible choices: `auto`, `classify`, `embed`, `none`

Convert the model using adapters defined in
|vllm.model_executor.models.adapters|. The most common use case is to
adapt a text generation model to be used for pooling tasks.

Default: `auto`

                                               *cli-run-batch-index-tokenizer*

--tokenizer

Name or path of the Hugging Face tokenizer to use. If unspecified, model
name or path will be used.

                                          *cli-run-batch-index-tokenizer-mode*

--tokenizer-mode

Possible choices: `alpamayo2_super`, `auto`, `cohere`, `deepseek_v32`,
`deepseek_v4`, `glm5_next`, `hf`, `hy_v4`, `inkling`, `kimi_k3`,
`mistral`

Tokenizer mode:

- "auto" will use the tokenizer from `mistral_common` for Mistral models
  if available, otherwise it will use the "hf" tokenizer.
- "hf" will use the fast tokenizer if available.
- "alpamayo2_super" uses the "hf" tokenizer with Alpamayo 2 Super's
  trajectory and special tokens added; selected automatically for
  `nvidia/Alpamayo2-Super`.
- "hy_v4" uses the HF tokenizer with HYV4 VL chat rendering.
- "mistral" will always use the tokenizer from `mistral_common`.
- "deepseek_v32" will always use the tokenizer from `deepseek_v32`.
- "deepseek_v4" will always use the tokenizer from `deepseek_v4`.
- "deepseek_v41" will use the DeepSeek V4.1 prompt encoder.
- "kimi_k3" requires a tokenizer with native Kimi K3 XTML chat encoding.
  It rejects the generic Hugging Face Jinja fallback.
- "glm5_next" uses the "hf" tokenizer and renderer on the Python
  frontend; the Rust frontend renders GLM-5.3 and GLM-5.3-Flash prompts
  natively, and selects this under "auto" for the `glm5_next` model type
  (GLM-5.3-Flash). GLM-5.3-Flash multimodal inputs require the Rust
  frontend; Python supports text-only inference with image and video
  limits set to zero.
- "cohere" uses the standard HF tokenizer but renders the chat template
  via the `cohere_melody` library (cmd3 / cmd4 templates) instead of
  Jinja, and surfaces grounded-citation metadata on responses.
- Other custom values can be supported via plugins.

To swap the Rust BPE backend that powers HF fast tokenizers for the
fastokens https://github.com/crusoecloud/fastokens implementation, set
`VLLM_USE_FASTOKENS=1` instead --- that override applies to any mode
that loads an HF fast tokenizer (`hf`, `deepseek_v32`, `deepseek_v4`,
...).

Default: `auto`

                  *cli-run-batch-index-trust-remote-code-no-trust-remote-code*
`--trust-remote-code`, `--no-trust-remote-code`

Trust remote code (e.g., from HuggingFace) when downloading the model
and tokenizer.

Default: `False`

                                                   *cli-run-batch-index-dtype*

--dtype

Possible choices: `auto`, `bfloat16`, `float`, `float16`, `float32`,
`half`

Data type for model weights and activations:

- "auto" will use FP16 precision for FP32 and FP16 models, and BF16
  precision for BF16 models.
- "half" for FP16. Recommended for AWQ quantization.
- "float16" is the same as "half".
- "bfloat16" for a balance between precision and range.
- "float" is shorthand for FP32 precision.
- "float32" for FP32 precision.

Default: `auto`

                                                    *cli-run-batch-index-seed*

--seed

Random seed for reproducibility.

The seed must be in the range [0, 2**32 - 1].

We must set the global seed because otherwise, different tensor parallel
workers would sample different tokens, leading to inconsistent results.

Default: `0`

                                          *cli-run-batch-index-hf-config-path*

--hf-config-path

Name or path of the Hugging Face config to use. If unspecified, model
name or path will be used.

                                *cli-run-batch-index-allowed-local-media-path*

--allowed-local-media-path

Allowing API requests to read local images or videos from directories
specified by the server file system. This is a security risk. Should
only be enabled in trusted environments.

Default: `""`

                                   *cli-run-batch-index-allowed-media-domains*

--allowed-media-domains

If set, only media URLs that belong to this domain can be used for
multi-modal inputs.

                                                *cli-run-batch-index-revision*

--revision

The specific model version to use. It can be a branch name, a tag name,
or a commit id. If unspecified, will use the default version.

                                           *cli-run-batch-index-code-revision*

--code-revision

The specific revision to use for the model code on the Hugging Face Hub.
It can be a branch name, a tag name, or a commit id. If unspecified,
will use the default version.

                                      *cli-run-batch-index-tokenizer-revision*

--tokenizer-revision

The specific revision to use for the tokenizer on the Hugging Face Hub.
It can be a branch name, a tag name, or a commit id. If unspecified,
will use the default version.

                                           *cli-run-batch-index-max-model-len*

--max-model-len

Model context length (prompt and output). If unspecified, will be
automatically derived from the model config.

When passing via `--max-model-len`, supports k/m/g/K/M/G in
human-readable format. Examples:

- 1k -> 1000
- 1K -> 1024
- 25.6k -> 25,600
- -1 or 'auto' -> Automatically choose the maximum model length that
  fits in GPU memory. This will use the model's maximum context length
  if it fits, otherwise it will find the largest length that can be
  accommodated.

Parse human-readable integers like '1k', '2M', etc. Including decimal
values with decimal multipliers. Also accepts -1 or 'auto' as a special
value for auto-detection.

>text
    Examples:
    - '1k' -> 1,000
    - '1K' -> 1,024
    - '25.6k' -> 25,600
    - '-1' or 'auto' -> -1 (special value for auto-detection)
<

`--quantization`, `-q`                    *cli-run-batch-index-quantization-q*

Method used to quantize the weights. If `None`, we first check the
`quantization_config` attribute in the model config file. If that is
`None`, we assume the model weights are not quantized and use `dtype` to
determine the data type of the weights.

                                     *cli-run-batch-index-quantization-config*

--quantization-config

User-facing quantization configuration. Carries per-layer-kind specs
(linear, moe) and ignore patterns; see
|vllm.config.quantization.QuantizationConfigArgs|. Auto-populated from
the matching online shorthand when `quantization` is one of the values
in `ONLINE_QUANT_SHORTHAND_NAMES`.

Should either be a valid JSON string or JSON keys passed individually.

*cli-run-batch-index-allow-deprecated-quantization-no-allow-deprecated-quantization*
`--allow-deprecated-quantization`, `--no-allow-deprecated-quantization`

Whether to allow deprecated quantization methods.

Default: `False`

                          *cli-run-batch-index-enforce-eager-no-enforce-eager*
`--enforce-eager`, `--no-enforce-eager`

Whether to always use eager-mode PyTorch. If True, we will disable CUDA
graph and always execute the model in eager mode. If False, we will use
CUDA graph and eager execution in hybrid for maximal performance and
flexibility.

NOTE: This disables both `torch.compile` and CUDA graphs, and is
equivalent to setting `-cc.mode=none -cc.cudagraph_mode=none`.

Default: `False`

*cli-run-batch-index-enable-return-routed-experts-disable-return-routed-experts*
`--enable-return-routed-experts`, `--disable-return-routed-experts`

Capture and return routed-experts auxiliary outputs.

Default: `False`

            *cli-run-batch-index-return-sampling-mask-no-return-sampling-mask*
`--return-sampling-mask`, `--no-return-sampling-mask`

Whether to return the post-processing token support for each sample.

Default: `False`

                                            *cli-run-batch-index-max-logprobs*

--max-logprobs

Maximum number of log probabilities to return when `logprobs` is
specified in |vllm.sampling_params.SamplingParams|. The default value
comes the default for the OpenAI Chat Completions API. -1 means no cap,
i.e. all (output_length * vocab_size) logprobs are allowed to be
returned and it may cause OOM.

Default: `20`

                                           *cli-run-batch-index-logprobs-mode*

--logprobs-mode

Possible choices: `processed_logits`, `processed_logprobs`,
`raw_logits`, `raw_logprobs`

Indicates the content returned in the logprobs and prompt_logprobs.
Supported mode: 1) raw_logprobs, 2) processed_logprobs, 3) raw_logits,
4) processed_logits. Raw means the values before applying any logit
processors, like bad words. Processed means the values after applying
all processors, including temperature and top_k/top_p. Note: for
prompt_logprobs, processed_ and raw_ yield identical results because
prompt tokens do not go through sampling processors.

Default: `raw_logprobs`

                      *cli-run-batch-index-use-fp64-gumbel-no-use-fp64-gumbel*
`--use-fp64-gumbel`, `--no-use-fp64-gumbel`

Whether to use FP64 (instead of FP32) random noise for Gumbel-max and
equivalent exponential-race sampling. FP64 preserves lower-tail sampling
events that fp32 uniform/exponential draws can truncate, at the cost of
significantly lower throughput on most GPUs.

Default: `False`

                *cli-run-batch-index-enable-trace-replay-disable-trace-replay*
`--enable-trace-replay`, `--disable-trace-replay`

Whether to allow requests to set
`SamplingParams.trace_decode_token_ids`, which forces decoding to follow
a predetermined token sequence while still computing real logprobs.
Reserved for debugging and RL workflows: enabling it reserves a
per-request trace buffer, so it is off by default.

Default: `False`

            *cli-run-batch-index-disable-sliding-window-enable-sliding-window*
`--disable-sliding-window`, `--enable-sliding-window`

Whether to disable sliding window. If True, we will disable the sliding
window functionality of the model, capping to sliding window size. If
the model does not support sliding window, this argument is ignored.

Default: `False`

              *cli-run-batch-index-skip-tokenizer-init-no-skip-tokenizer-init*
`--skip-tokenizer-init`, `--no-skip-tokenizer-init`

Skip initialization of tokenizer and detokenizer. Expects valid
`prompt_token_ids` and `None` for prompt from the input. The generated
output will contain token ids.

Default: `False`

              *cli-run-batch-index-enable-prompt-embeds-disable-prompt-embeds*
`--enable-prompt-embeds`, `--disable-prompt-embeds`

If `True`, enables passing text embeddings as inputs via the
`prompt_embeds` key.

WARNING: The vLLM engine may crash if incorrect shape of embeddings is
passed. Only enable this flag for trusted users!

Default: `False`

                                       *cli-run-batch-index-served-model-name*

--served-model-name

The model name(s) used in the API. If multiple names are provided, the
server will respond to any of the provided names. The model name in the
model field of a response will be the first name in this list. If not
specified, the model name will be the same as the `--model` argument.
Noted that this name(s) will also be used in `model_name` tag content of
prometheus metrics, if multiple names provided, metrics tag will take
the first one.

                                           *cli-run-batch-index-config-format*

--config-format

Possible choices: `auto`, `hf`, `mistral`

The format of the model config to load:

- "auto" will try to load the config in hf format if available after
  trying to load in mistral format.
- "hf" will load the config in hf format.
- "mistral" will load the config in mistral format.

Default: `auto`

                                                *cli-run-batch-index-hf-token*

--hf-token

The token to use as HTTP bearer authorization for remote files . If
`True`, will use the token generated when running `hf auth login`
(stored in `~/.cache/huggingface/token`).

                                            *cli-run-batch-index-hf-overrides*

--hf-overrides

If a dictionary, contains arguments to be forwarded to the Hugging Face
config. If a callable, it is called to update the HuggingFace config.

Default: `{}`

                                   *cli-run-batch-index-model-class-overrides*

--model-class-overrides

Override the model class used for one or more architectures, mapping the
architecture name to a `"module:class"` target (the same format accepted
by `ModelRegistry.register_model`). This registers the target class at
runtime, e.g.
`{"GlmMoeDsaForCausalLM": "vllm.models.deepseek_v32.nvidia.model:DeepseekV32ForCausalLM"}`.
This argument is for development and debugging purposes only.

Should either be a valid JSON string or JSON keys passed individually.

Default: `{}`

                                           *cli-run-batch-index-pooler-config*

--pooler-config

Pooler config which controls the behaviour of output pooling in pooling
models.

API docs: |vllm.config.PoolerConfig|

Should either be a valid JSON string or JSON keys passed individually.

                                       *cli-run-batch-index-generation-config*

--generation-config

The folder path to the generation config. Defaults to `"auto"`, the
generation config will be loaded from model path. If set to `"vllm"`, no
generation config is loaded, vLLM defaults will be used. If set to a
folder path, the generation config will be loaded from the specified
folder path. If `max_new_tokens` is specified in generation config, then
it sets a server-wide limit on the number of output tokens for all
requests.

Default: `auto`

                              *cli-run-batch-index-override-generation-config*
`--override-generation-config`

Overrides or sets generation config. e.g. `{"temperature": 0.5}`. If
used with `--generation-config auto`, the override parameters will be
merged with the default config from the model. If used with
`--generation-config vllm`, only the override parameters are used.

Should either be a valid JSON string or JSON keys passed individually.

Default: `{}`

                    *cli-run-batch-index-enable-sleep-mode-disable-sleep-mode*
`--enable-sleep-mode`, `--disable-sleep-mode`

Enable sleep mode for the engine (only cuda and hip platforms are
supported).

Default: `False`

                                      *cli-run-batch-index-sleep-mode-backend*

--sleep-mode-backend

Mechanism used to free and restore GPU state for sleep mode. `"cumem"`
(default) uses the built-in |vllm.device_allocator.cumem.CuMemAllocator|
and is behavior-compatible with prior releases. Additional backends
(CUDA checkpoint, CRIU, durable snapshot) may be registered in-tree or
by plugins (RFC #34303).

Default: `cumem`

*cli-run-batch-index-sleep-mode-offload-cudagraph-no-sleep-mode-offload-cudagraph*
`--sleep-mode-offload-cudagraph`, `--no-sleep-mode-offload-cudagraph`

Back up CUDA graph memory to CPU during sleep, restored in place on
wake. Takes effect with enable_sleep_mode, the cumem backend and CUDA
graphs.

Default: `False`

                          *cli-run-batch-index-sleep-preserve-parameter-names*
`--sleep-preserve-parameter-names`

Parameter-name globs to preserve across level-2 sleep. Use target globs
for frozen Engram table weights and scales omitted by the trainer (e.g.
`*.engram.embed_tokens.weight*`). Use `draft.<name>` for draft-private
parameters, or `draft.*` for all. The sender must omit these parameters;
loaders must preserve their storage.

Default: `[]`

                                        *cli-run-batch-index-masked-token-ids*

--masked-token-ids

Token ids that are never sampled (their logits are set to -inf before
sampling), e.g. the lm_head rows past the tokenizer or placeholder
tokens. Prompt logprobs are unaffected.

          *cli-run-batch-index-enable-cumem-allocator-disable-cumem-allocator*
`--enable-cumem-allocator`, `--disable-cumem-allocator`

Enable the custom cumem allocator to leverage advanced GPU memory
allocation features such as multi-node NVLink support.

Sleep mode automatically enables this allocator. Only cuda and hip
platforms are supported.

Default: `False`

      *cli-run-batch-index-enable-nccl-comm-suspend-disable-nccl-comm-suspend*
`--enable-nccl-comm-suspend`, `--disable-nccl-comm-suspend`

Enable releasing NCCL communicator memory during sleep mode
(`ncclCommSuspend`/`ncclCommResume`). Experimental; when disabled (the
default) sleep still releases weights/KV-cache memory as before.

Default: `False`

*cli-run-batch-index-checkpoint-prepare-on-startup-no-checkpoint-prepare-on-startup*
`--checkpoint-prepare-on-startup`, `--no-checkpoint-prepare-on-startup`

Prepare each engine for a process checkpoint once it starts, as
`/checkpoint/prepare` does. It serves only after `/checkpoint/restore`.
Requires sleep mode.

Default: `False`

                                              *cli-run-batch-index-model-impl*

--model-impl

Possible choices: `auto`, `terratorch`, `transformers`, `vllm`

Which implementation of the model to use:

- "auto" will try to use the vLLM implementation, if it exists, and fall
  back to the Transformers implementation if no vLLM implementation is
  available.
- "vllm" will use the vLLM model implementation.
- "transformers" will use the Transformers model implementation.
- "terratorch" will use the TerraTorch model implementation.

Default: `auto`

                                       *cli-run-batch-index-logits-processors*

--logits-processors

One or more logits processors' fully-qualified class names or class
definitions

                                     *cli-run-batch-index-io-processor-plugin*

--io-processor-plugin

IOProcessor plugin name to load at model startup

                                    *cli-run-batch-index-renderer-num-workers*

--renderer-num-workers

Number of worker threads in the renderer thread pool. The pool is
consumed by the async renderer path (e.g. the OpenAI-compatible API
server started by `vllm serve`) to parallelize tokenization, chat
template rendering, and multimodal preprocessing across concurrent
requests.

The offline |vllm.entrypoints.llm.LLM| entrypoint uses the synchronous
renderer path and processes prompts (including multimodal preprocessing)
serially, so this setting has no effect there.

Default: `1`

LoadConfig                                    *cli-run-batch-index-loadconfig*

Configuration for loading the model weights.

                                             *cli-run-batch-index-load-format*

--load-format

The format of the model weights to load.

- "auto" will try to load the weights in the safetensors format and fall
  back to the pytorch bin format if safetensors format is not available.
- "pt" will load the weights in the pytorch bin format.
- "safetensors" will load the weights in the safetensors format.
- "instanttensor" will load the Safetensors weights on CUDA devices
  using InstantTensor, which enables distributed loading with pipelined
  prefetching and fast direct I/O.
- "ipc_cache" will map post-quantized weights from a local weight cache
  daemon via CUDA IPC for fast engine restarts. Launch the daemon with
  `vllm preload` first.
- "peer_cache" will build the model on meta and pull post-processed
  weights from a source replica's weight cache daemon. Set internally by
  `vllm preload --weight-cache-seed`; not for engine use.
- "npcache" will load the weights in pytorch format and store a numpy
  cache to speed up the loading.
- "dummy" will initialize the weights with random values, which is
  mainly for profiling.
- "tensorizer" will use CoreWeave's tensorizer library for fast weight
  loading. See the Tensorize vLLM Model script in the Examples section
  for more information.
- "runai_streamer" will load the Safetensors weights using Run:ai Model
  Streamer.
- "runai_streamer_sharded" will load weights from pre-sharded checkpoint
  files using Run:ai Model Streamer.
- "sharded_state" will load weights from pre-sharded checkpoint files,
  supporting efficient loading of tensor-parallel models.
- "mistral" will load weights from consolidated safetensors files used
  by Mistral models.
- "modelexpress" will load weights using ModelExpress.
- Other custom values can be supported via plugins.

Default: `auto`

                                            *cli-run-batch-index-download-dir*

--download-dir

Directory to download and load the weights, default to the default cache
directory of Hugging Face.

                               *cli-run-batch-index-safetensors-load-strategy*

--safetensors-load-strategy

Possible choices: `eager`, |vllm.logging_utils.lazy.lazy|, `prefetch`,
`torchao`, `None`

Specifies the loading strategy for safetensors weights.

- None (default): Uses memory-mapped (lazy) loading. When an NFS
  filesystem is detected and the total checkpoint size fits within 90%
  of available RAM, prefetching is enabled automatically.
- "lazy": Weights are memory-mapped from the file. This enables
  on-demand loading and is highly efficient for models on local storage.
  Unlike the default (None), auto-prefetch on NFS is not performed.
- "eager": The entire file is read into CPU memory upfront before
  loading. This is recommended for models on network filesystems (e.g.,
  Lustre, NFS) as it avoids inefficient random reads, significantly
  speeding up model initialization. However, it uses more CPU RAM.
- "prefetch": Checkpoint files are read into the OS page cache before
  workers load them, speeding up the model loading phase. Useful on
  network or high-latency storage.
- "torchao": Weights are loaded in upfront and then reconstructed into
  torchao tensor subclasses. This is used when the checkpoint was
  quantized using torchao and saved using safetensors. Needs
  `torchao >= 0.14.0`.

                        *cli-run-batch-index-safetensors-prefetch-num-threads*
`--safetensors-prefetch-num-threads`

Number of worker threads used to prefetch safetensors checkpoint files
into the OS page cache when safetensors prefetching is enabled.

Default: `8`

                         *cli-run-batch-index-safetensors-prefetch-block-size*
`--safetensors-prefetch-block-size`

Read size in bytes for each safetensors checkpoint file prefetch.

Parse human-readable integers like '1k', '2M', etc. Including decimal
values with decimal multipliers.

>text
    Examples:
    - '1k' -> 1,000
    - '1K' -> 1,024
    - '25.6k' -> 25,600
<
Default: `16777216`

                               *cli-run-batch-index-model-loader-extra-config*

--model-loader-extra-config

Extra config for model loader. This will be passed to the model loader
corresponding to the chosen load_format.

Default: `{}`

                                         *cli-run-batch-index-ignore-patterns*

--ignore-patterns

The list of patterns to ignore when loading the model. Default to
"original/*/" to avoid repeated loading of llama's checkpoints.

Default: `['original/**/*']`

                    *cli-run-batch-index-use-tqdm-on-load-no-use-tqdm-on-load*
`--use-tqdm-on-load`, `--no-use-tqdm-on-load`

Whether to enable tqdm for showing progress bar when loading model
weights.

Default: `True`

                                    *cli-run-batch-index-pt-load-map-location*

--pt-load-map-location

The map location for loading pytorch checkpoint, to support loading
checkpoints can only be loaded on certain devices like "cuda", this is
equivalent to `{"": "cuda"}`. Another supported format is mapping from
different devices like from GPU 1 to GPU 0: `{"cuda:1": "cuda:0"}`. Note
that when passed from command line, the strings in dictionary need to be
double quoted for json parsing. For more details, see the original doc
for `map_location` parameter in `torch.load`
https://pytorch.org/docs/stable/generated/torch.load.html#torch.load
parameter.

Default: `cpu`

  *cli-run-batch-index-shuffle-safetensors-files-no-shuffle-safetensors-files*
`--shuffle-safetensors-files`, `--no-shuffle-safetensors-files`

Whether to shuffle the safetensors checkpoint files during loading. When
enabled, files are randomly shuffled based on the distributed rank,
which can help with load balancing and improve weight loading speed in
startup scenarios. This reduces I/O contention by ensuring different
ranks load different files in a randomized order. Set to True to enable
shuffling. Default is False.

Default: `False`

AttentionConfig                          *cli-run-batch-index-attentionconfig*

Configuration for attention mechanisms in vLLM.

                                       *cli-run-batch-index-attention-backend*

--attention-backend

Attention backend to use. Use "auto" or None for automatic selection.
TORCH_SDPA is reserved for multi-modal encoder attention.

MambaConfig                                  *cli-run-batch-index-mambaconfig*

Configuration for Mamba SSM backends.

                                           *cli-run-batch-index-mamba-backend*

--mamba-backend

Mamba SSU backend to use.

Default: `MambaBackendEnum.TRITON`

                                     *cli-run-batch-index-mamba-ssu-algorithm*

--mamba-ssu-algorithm

Possible choices: `auto`, `horizontal`, `simple`, `vertical`, `None`

Selective state update algorithm to use with the FlashInfer backend.
None defaults to FlashInfer's "auto" algorithm. Forced algorithms must
be supported by FlashInfer for the active GPU, state dtype, and decoding
mode.

                                   *cli-run-batch-index-mamba-prefill-backend*

--mamba-prefill-backend

Kernel backend for the SSD scan of Mamba2 prefills. "flashinfer" runs
FlashInfer's fused SSD scan (SM100/SM103 only); prefills that store
Mamba checkpoints always use "triton".

Default: `MambaBackendEnum.TRITON`

*cli-run-batch-index-enable-mamba-cache-stochastic-rounding-disable-mamba-cache-stochastic-rounding*
`--enable-mamba-cache-stochastic-rounding`,
`--disable-mamba-cache-stochastic-rounding`

Enable stochastic rounding when writing SSM state to fp16 cache. Uses
random bits to unbias the rounding error, which can improve numerical
stability for long sequences. The Triton backend requires compute
capability 10.0 on CUDA or gfx950 on ROCm.

Default: `False`

                               *cli-run-batch-index-mamba-cache-philox-rounds*

--mamba-cache-philox-rounds

Number of Philox PRNG rounds for stochastic rounding random number
generation. 0 uses the Triton default. Higher values improve randomness
quality at the cost of compute.

Default: `0`

StructuredOutputsConfig          *cli-run-batch-index-structuredoutputsconfig*

Dataclass which contains structured outputs config for the engine.

--reasoning-parser

Select the reasoning parser depending on the model that you're using.

`none` disables reasoning parsing, the same as leaving it unset.

                                 *cli-run-batch-index-reasoning-parser-plugin*

--reasoning-parser-plugin

Path to a dynamically reasoning parser plugin that can be dynamically

ParallelConfig                            *cli-run-batch-index-parallelconfig*

Configuration for the distributed execution.

                            *cli-run-batch-index-distributed-executor-backend*
`--distributed-executor-backend`

Possible choices: `external_launcher`, `mp`, `ray`, `uni`

Backend to use for distributed model workers, either "ray" or "mp"
(multiprocessing). If the product of pipeline_parallel_size and
tensor_parallel_size is less than or equal to the number of GPUs
available, "mp" will be used to keep processing on a single host.
Otherwise, an error will be raised. To use "mp" you must also set
nnodes, and to use "ray" you must manually set
distributed_executor_backend to "ray".

Note: TPU https://docs.vllm.ai/projects/tpu/en/latest/ platform only
supports Ray for distributed inference.

                               *cli-run-batch-index-pipeline-parallel-size-pp*
`--pipeline-parallel-size`, `-pp`

Number of pipeline parallel groups.

Default: `1`

                                             *cli-run-batch-index-master-addr*

--master-addr

distributed master address for multi-node distributed inference when
distributed_executor_backend is mp.

Default: `127.0.0.1`

                                             *cli-run-batch-index-master-port*

--master-port

distributed master port for multi-node distributed inference when
distributed_executor_backend is mp.

Default: `29501`

`--nnodes`, `-n`                                *cli-run-batch-index-nnodes-n*

num of nodes for multi-node distributed inference when
distributed_executor_backend is mp.

Default: `1`

`--node-rank`, `-r`                          *cli-run-batch-index-node-rank-r*

distributed node rank for multi-node distributed inference when
distributed_executor_backend is mp.

Default: `0`

                             *cli-run-batch-index-distributed-timeout-seconds*
`--distributed-timeout-seconds`

Timeout in seconds for distributed operations (e.g.,
init_process_group). If set, this value is passed to
torch.distributed.init_process_group as the timeout parameter. If None,
PyTorch's default timeout is used (600s for NCCL). Increase this for
multi-node setups where model downloads may be slow.

                         *cli-run-batch-index-cpu-distributed-timeout-seconds*
`--cpu-distributed-timeout-seconds`

Timeout (in seconds) for cpu communication groups. If None, PyTorch's
default timeout is used (1800s for gloo).

`--numa-bind`, `--no-numa-bind`   *cli-run-batch-index-numa-bind-no-numa-bind*

Enable NUMA binding for GPU worker subprocesses.

Workers are pinned to their GPU's NUMA-local CPUs; on PCT-capable Xeons
they also auto-bind to the SKU's PCT priority cores.

If None (default), vLLM binds only when the environment supports it
(multiple NUMA nodes, `numactl` installed, unrestricted CPU affinity, a
default NUMA memory policy, and the `spawn` start method with an mp/uni
executor and mp DP, including PCP). Ray, external launchers and
device-ID remapping are excluded from automatic binding. Automatic mode
prefers GPU-local memory but permits allocations on other nodes.
Unsupported environments start unbound. With `True`, strict memory
binding may override an existing policy, and a missing `numactl` or
undetectable GPU-to-NUMA topology is a startup error; `False` disables
binding.

                                         *cli-run-batch-index-numa-bind-nodes*

--numa-bind-nodes

NUMA node to bind each GPU worker to.

Setting this list implies explicit `numa_bind=True` and requests strict
`--membind`, not automatic soft memory preference. It cannot be combined
with `numa_bind=False`.

Specify one NUMA node per visible GPU, for example `[0, 0, 1, 1]` for a
4-GPU system with GPUs 0-1 on NUMA node 0 and GPUs 2-3 on NUMA node 1.
If unset and `numa_bind=True`, vLLM auto-detects the GPU-to-NUMA
topology. The values are passed to `numactl --membind` and
`--cpunodebind`, so they must be valid `numactl` NUMA node indices.

                                          *cli-run-batch-index-numa-bind-cpus*

--numa-bind-cpus

Optional CPU lists to bind each GPU worker to.

Setting this list implies explicit `numa_bind=True` and requests strict
`--membind`, not CPU-only binding or automatic soft memory preference.
It cannot be combined with `numa_bind=False`.

Specify one CPU list per visible GPU, for example
`["0-3", "4-7", "8-11", "12-15"]`. When set, vLLM uses
`numactl --physcpubind` instead of `--cpunodebind`. This is useful for
custom policies such as binding to PCT or other high-frequency cores.
Each entry must use `numactl --physcpubind` CPU-list syntax, for example
`"0-3"` or `"0,2,4-7"`.

                                              *cli-run-batch-index-device-ids*

--device-ids

Comma-separated physical GPU device IDs or UUIDs to use (e.g.
--device-ids "2,3,5,7"). Avoids setting CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES, preserving
full GPU topology visibility for GPU-NIC affinity and DeepGEMM. Note:
has no effect with Ray executors; use Ray placement groups for GPU
selection instead.

`--tensor-parallel-size`, `-tp`  *cli-run-batch-index-tensor-parallel-size-tp*

Number of tensor parallel groups.

Default: `1`

                        *cli-run-batch-index-decode-context-parallel-size-dcp*
`--decode-context-parallel-size`, `-dcp`

Number of ranks that shard the decode KV cache. DCP does not expand the
process world size. Without PCP, DCP reuses TP ranks. With PCP, DCP
either spans the PCP axis or the full TP x PCP block.

Default: `1`

                                        *cli-run-batch-index-dcp-comm-backend*

--dcp-comm-backend

Possible choices: `a2a`, `ag_rs`, `None`

Communication backend for Decode Context Parallel (DCP). - "ag_rs":
AllGather + ReduceScatter (existing behavior) - "a2a": All-to-All
exchange of partial outputs + LSE, then combine with Triton kernel.
Reduces NCCL calls from 3 to 2 per layer for MLA models.

`None` selects the model default, which is "ag_rs" unless the model
overrides it via |vllm.config.ParallelConfig.set_dcp_defaults|.

                      *cli-run-batch-index-dcp-q-replicate-no-dcp-q-replicate*
`--dcp-q-replicate`, `--no-dcp-q-replicate`

Replicate the MLA query projection within each DCP group so decode can
skip the query all-gather.

With DCP the KV cache is sharded across the group, so the standard MLA
decode path all-gathers the query every step. Replicating the (small)
query projection at load time lets each rank materialize the full
group-local head set and skip that collective, at the cost of computing
the projection redundantly on every rank in the group.

                            *cli-run-batch-index-dcp-kv-cache-interleave-size*
`--dcp-kv-cache-interleave-size`

Interleave size of kv_cache storage while using DCP.
dcp_kv_cache_interleave_size has been replaced by
cp_kv_cache_interleave_size, and will be deprecated when PCP is fully
supported.

Default: `1`

                             *cli-run-batch-index-cp-kv-cache-interleave-size*
`--cp-kv-cache-interleave-size`

Interleave size of kv_cache storage while using DCP. Store
interleave_size tokens on dcp_rank i, then store next interleave_size
tokens on dcp_rank i+1. Interleave_size=1: token-level alignment, where
token `i` is stored on dcp_rank `i % dcp_world_size`.
Interleave_size=block_size: block-level alignment, where tokens are
first populated to the preceding ranks. Tokens are then stored in (rank
i+1, block j) only after (rank i, block j) is fully occupied. Block_size
should be greater than or equal to cp_kv_cache_interleave_size.
Block_size should be divisible by cp_kv_cache_interleave_size.

When --cp-kv-cache-interleave-size is omitted (None), the interleave
size is resolved automatically based on NIXL transfer requirements.
Explicit settings take priority.

                       *cli-run-batch-index-prefill-context-parallel-size-pcp*
`--prefill-context-parallel-size`, `-pcp`

Number of ranks that split prefill sequence computation. PCP expands the
process world size but does not increase the KV-cache shard count.

Default: `1`

`--data-parallel-size`, `-dp`      *cli-run-batch-index-data-parallel-size-dp*

Number of data parallel groups. MoE layers will be sharded according to
the product of the tensor, prefill-context, and data parallel sizes.

Default: `1`

`--data-parallel-rank`, `-dpn`    *cli-run-batch-index-data-parallel-rank-dpn*

Data parallel rank of this instance. When set, enables external load
balancer mode for MoE data-parallel deployments. Unsupported for non-MoE
models; launch independent vLLM instances instead.

                            *cli-run-batch-index-data-parallel-start-rank-dpr*
`--data-parallel-start-rank`, `-dpr`

Starting data parallel rank for secondary nodes.

                            *cli-run-batch-index-data-parallel-size-local-dpl*
`--data-parallel-size-local`, `-dpl`

Number of data parallel replicas to run on this node.

                               *cli-run-batch-index-data-parallel-address-dpa*
`--data-parallel-address`, `-dpa`

Address of data parallel cluster head-node.

                              *cli-run-batch-index-data-parallel-rpc-port-dpp*
`--data-parallel-rpc-port`, `-dpp`

Fixed port for data parallel RPC communication. All nodes must use the
same port.

                               *cli-run-batch-index-data-parallel-backend-dpb*
`--data-parallel-backend`, `-dpb`

Backend for data parallel, either "mp" or "ray".

Default: `mp`

  *cli-run-batch-index-data-parallel-hybrid-lb-no-data-parallel-hybrid-lb-dph*
`--data-parallel-hybrid-lb`, `--no-data-parallel-hybrid-lb`, `-dph`

Whether to use "hybrid" DP LB mode. Applies only to online serving and
when data_parallel_size > 0. Enables running an AsyncLLM and API server
on a "per-node" basis where vLLM load balances between local data
parallel ranks, but an external LB balances between vLLM nodes/replicas.
Set explicitly in conjunction with --data-parallel-start-rank.

Default: `False`

*cli-run-batch-index-data-parallel-external-lb-no-data-parallel-external-lb-dpe*
`--data-parallel-external-lb`, `--no-data-parallel-external-lb`, `-dpe`

Whether to use "external" DP LB mode. Applies only to online serving and
when data_parallel_size > 0. This is useful for a "one-pod-per-rank"
wide-EP setup in Kubernetes. Supported only for MoE deployments; non-MoE
models should use independent vLLM instances without --data-parallel-*
arguments. Set implicitly when --data-parallel-rank is provided
explicitly to vllm serve.

Default: `False`

                *cli-run-batch-index-data-parallel-multi-port-external-lb-dpm*
`--data-parallel-multi-port-external-lb`, `-dpm`

Run a node-local supervisor that launches one external-LB API server per
local data parallel rank and exposes aggregated health on a supervisor
port.

Default: `False`

                              *cli-run-batch-index-enable-hc-sp-disable-hc-sp*
`--enable-hc-sp`, `--disable-hc-sp`

Enable sequence parallelism for HyperConnection (HC) computation.
Currently supported by Qwen4Exp on CUDA/ROCm with TP>1 and PP=1 (ignored
when TP=1). Does not change MoE parallelism. MoE SP may enable HC SP
independently of this flag.

Default: `False`

       *cli-run-batch-index-enable-expert-parallel-ep-disable-expert-parallel*
`--enable-expert-parallel`, `-ep`, `--disable-expert-parallel`

Use expert parallelism instead of tensor parallelism for MoE layers.

Default: `False`

*cli-run-batch-index-enable-batch-sharded-sampling-disable-batch-sharded-sampling*
`--enable-batch-sharded-sampling`, `--disable-batch-sharded-sampling`

Use sharded sampling across tensor parallel ranks. Each rank samples a
slice of the batch instead of every rank sampling all of it. Currently
defaults to False if not set. Enabling it explicitly raises when the
config cannot support it (`tensor_parallel_size` must be > 1,
`max_num_seqs` at least `tensor_parallel_size`, and `max_logprobs`
non-negative). Models opt in by implementing `compute_logits_local`.

        *cli-run-batch-index-enable-ep-weight-filter-disable-ep-weight-filter*
`--enable-ep-weight-filter`, `--disable-ep-weight-filter`

Skip non-local expert weights during model loading when expert
parallelism is active. Each rank only reads its own expert shard from
disk, which can drastically reduce storage I/O for MoE models with
per-expert weight tensors (e.g. DeepSeek, Mixtral, Kimi-K2.5). With
EPLB, each rank also reads the experts its redundant slots replicate in
the initial placement; elastic EP loads every expert. Has no effect on
3D fused-expert checkpoints (e.g. GPT-OSS) or non-MoE models.

Default: `False`

                                         *cli-run-batch-index-all2all-backend*

--all2all-backend

Possible choices: `allgather_reducescatter`, `alltoall_batched`, `auto`,
`deepep_high_throughput`, `deepep_low_latency`, `deepep_v2`,
`flashinfer_all2allv`, `flashinfer_nvlink_one_sided`,
`flashinfer_nvlink_two_sided`, `moonep`, `mori`, `mori_high_throughput`,
`mori_low_latency`, `naive`, `nixl_ep`, `pplx`

All2All backend for MoE expert parallel communication. Available
options:

- "auto": "flashinfer_nvlink_one_sided" for expert parallelism without
  elastic EP, with any data-parallel size on GB200/GB300/VR200 or on a
  single HGX B200/B300 node launched by vLLM (any parallel layout), for
  BF16/FP16, ModelOpt NVFP4 or 128x128 block FP8 models without LoRA or
  a MoE-EP backend; "allgather_reducescatter" otherwise
- "allgather_reducescatter": All2all based on allgather and
  reducescatter
- "alltoall_batched": All2all based on all_to_all_single, routing each
  token only to the ranks owning its experts, in batched activation
  format
- "deepep_high_throughput": Use deepep high-throughput kernels
- "deepep_low_latency": Use deepep low-latency kernels
- "mori_high_throughput": MoRI EP with InterNodeV1 for multi-node
  (deprecated; use "mori")
- "mori_low_latency": MoRI EP with InterNodeV1LL for multi-node
  (deprecated; use "mori")
- "moonep": MoonEP balanced EP with dynamic redundant experts (NVLink)
- "mori": MoRI EP; uses EPv2 where supported (see `VLLM_MORI_EP_V2`) and
  EPv1 otherwise, picking the EPv1 inter-node kernel from the token
  budget (see `VLLM_MORI_DISPATCH_INTER_KERNEL_SWITCH_THRESHOLD`)
- "nixl_ep": Use nixl-ep kernels
- "flashinfer_nvlink_one_sided": Use flashinfer high-throughput a2a
  kernels
- "flashinfer_nvlink_two_sided": Use flashinfer two-sided kernels for
  mnnvl
- "passthrough": No all2all at all: the MoE backend dispatches and
  combines itself (e.g. the FlashInfer MoE-EP megakernels). Bound
  automatically for such MoE backends; not selectable from the CLI.

Default: `auto`

`--enable-dbo`, `--disable-dbo`   *cli-run-batch-index-enable-dbo-disable-dbo*

Enable dual batch overlap for the model executor.

Default: `False`

        *cli-run-batch-index-enable-shm-tensor-arena-disable-shm-tensor-arena*
`--enable-shm-tensor-arena`, `--disable-shm-tensor-arena`

Route large CPU tensors (e.g. multimodal `pixel_values`) in the
engine→worker broadcast through a zero-copy shared-memory arena instead
of sending a copy to every reader. Experimental: may be reconciled with
vLLM's existing multimodal shm tensor caching in a future release.
Opt-in; most beneficial for multimodal serving with large images and
tensor parallelism. Active only when all queue readers are node-local;
reserves slots in `/dev/shm`.

Default: `False`

                                             *cli-run-batch-index-ubatch-size*

--ubatch-size

Number of ubatch size.

Default: `0`

                    *cli-run-batch-index-enable-elastic-ep-disable-elastic-ep*
`--enable-elastic-ep`, `--disable-elastic-ep`

Enable elastic expert parallelism with stateless NCCL groups for DP/EP.

Default: `False`

                                  *cli-run-batch-index-elastic-ep-max-dp-size*

--elastic-ep-max-dp-size

Maximum data parallel size supported by elastic expert parallelism.

                              *cli-run-batch-index-dbo-decode-token-threshold*
`--dbo-decode-token-threshold`

The threshold for dual batch overlap for batches only containing
decodes. If the number of tokens in the request is greater than this
threshold, microbatching will be used. Otherwise, the request will be
processed in a single batch.

Default: `32`

                                        *cli-run-batch-index-dp-sync-interval*

--dp-sync-interval

Steps between DP finish-sync all-reduces; must match across DP ranks.

Default: `16`

                             *cli-run-batch-index-dbo-prefill-token-threshold*
`--dbo-prefill-token-threshold`

The threshold for dual batch overlap for batches that contain one or
more prefills. If the number of tokens in the request is greater than
this threshold, microbatching will be used. Otherwise, the request will
be processed in a single batch.

Default: `512`

                                            *cli-run-batch-index-dbo-schedule*

--dbo-schedule

Possible choices: `all-reduce-overlap`, `dispatch-combine-overlap`,
`None`

What one microbatch overlaps with the other's compute:
`dispatch-combine-overlap` its expert all-to-all, `all-reduce-overlap`
its TP all-reduces. `all-reduce-overlap` needs tensor parallelism on one
DP rank without expert parallelism and is the default there; everywhere
else the default is `dispatch-combine-overlap`.

*cli-run-batch-index-disable-nccl-for-dp-synchronization-enable-nccl-for-dp-synchronization*
`--disable-nccl-for-dp-synchronization`,
`--enable-nccl-for-dp-synchronization`

Forces the dp synchronization logic in vllm/v1/worker/dp_utils.py to use
Gloo instead of NCCL for its all reduce.

Defaults to True when async scheduling is enabled, False otherwise.

                                *cli-run-batch-index-enable-eplb-disable-eplb*
`--enable-eplb`, `--disable-eplb`

Enable expert parallelism load balancing for MoE layers.

Default: `False`

                                             *cli-run-batch-index-eplb-config*

--eplb-config

Expert parallelism configuration.

API docs: |vllm.config.EPLBConfig|

Should either be a valid JSON string or JSON keys passed individually.

Default:
`EPLBConfig(window_size=1000, step_interval=3000, num_redundant_experts=None, draft_num_redundant_experts=None, log_balancedness=False, log_balancedness_interval=1, use_async=True, policy=None, communicator=None, enable_migration_batching=False)`

                               *cli-run-batch-index-expert-placement-strategy*

--expert-placement-strategy

Possible choices: `linear`, `round_robin`

The expert placement strategy for MoE layers:

- "linear": Experts are placed in a contiguous manner. For example, with
  4 experts and 2 ranks, rank 0 will have experts [0, 1] and rank 1 will
  have experts [2, 3].
- "round_robin": Experts are placed in a round-robin manner. For
  example, with 4 experts and 2 ranks, rank 0 will have experts [0, 2]
  and rank 1 will have experts [1, 3]. This strategy can help improve
  load balancing for grouped expert models with no redundant experts.

Default: `linear`

                            *cli-run-batch-index-max-parallel-loading-workers*
`--max-parallel-loading-workers`

Maximum number of parallel loading workers when loading model
sequentially in multiple batches. To avoid RAM OOM when using tensor
parallel and large models.

        *cli-run-batch-index-ray-workers-use-nsight-no-ray-workers-use-nsight*
`--ray-workers-use-nsight`, `--no-ray-workers-use-nsight`

Whether to profile Ray workers with nsight, see
https://docs.ray.io/en/latest/ray-observability/user-guides/profiling.html#profiling-nsight-profiler.

Default: `False`

      *cli-run-batch-index-disable-custom-all-reduce-enable-custom-all-reduce*
`--disable-custom-all-reduce`, `--enable-custom-all-reduce`

Disable the custom all-reduce kernel and fall back to NCCL.

                                              *cli-run-batch-index-worker-cls*

--worker-cls

will be determined based on the platform.

Default: `auto`

                                    *cli-run-batch-index-worker-extension-cls*

--worker-extension-cls

The full name of the worker extension class to use. The worker extension
class is dynamically inherited by the worker class. This is used to
inject new attributes and methods to the worker class for use in
collective_rpc calls. It may also define `init_worker_extension(self)`,
called after device and distributed initialization but before model
loading, and `destroy_worker_extension(self)`, called (best-effort)
before the worker shuts down.

Default: `""`

          *cli-run-batch-index-enable-fault-tolerance-disable-fault-tolerance*
